# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.

How to decide whether to verify memory:

- Do not present unverified memory-derived facts as confirmed-current.

## User Profile

Joshua works extensively on LSATU/student learning experiences, LSAT teaching records, and Windows/AI-tool workflows. He uses Codex for implementation, independent review, recovery, connected-app drafting, and interactive UI inspection. He values grounded evidence, clear student-facing teaching, and explicit separation between local verification and live acceptance. His writing is concise, practical, warm, and typically signed “Best, Joshua.”

## User preferences

- Keep connected-app drafts preview-only when asked: “Show the draft here; do not send, post, or modify anything.” Match demonstrated writing style using concrete examples.
- Do not fabricate fixtures, outage evidence, deployment status, or acceptance. Keep tickets open until permissions, error states, responsive UI, and live task walks are evidenced.
- For independent reviews, give concrete ID, severity, path, failure scenario, fix, coverage, and limitations; do not edit/test/self-certify when prohibited.
- Student-facing teaching should be concept-first, visual, ordinary-language, and precise about complete conditions/modality; start from the student’s actual sticking point.
- After UI work, expose the result in a running Codex/browser preview when feasible.
- For estimates, distinguish confirmed balance, expiry evidence, and assumptions. Explain tools plainly when requested.
- Preserve user work: do not overwrite local conflicts, unrelated dirty files, or recovery data; verify before disruptive operations.

## General Tips

- Treat local tests/typecheck/build, deploy, and signed-in live acceptance as separate gates.
- On resume, cross-check handoffs against current status sources such as `wiki/hot.md` and the current diff before acting.
- On Windows, use native PowerShell/search tools unless a specific Git Bash/toolchain path is known; sandboxed app-data paths may be shadow copies.
- For interactive learning UI, test focus transitions, keyboard use, live-region feedback, retries, and real device/screen-reader cases separately.
- For recovery, hash/test archives, SQLite-check staged databases, preserve rollback copies, close writers, and wait for a completion receipt.

<collaboration_mode># Collaboration Mode: Default

You are now in Default mode. Any previous instructions for other modes (e.g. Plan mode) are no longer active.

Your active mode changes only when new developer instructions with a different `<collaboration_mode>...</collaboration_mode>` change it; user requests or tool descriptions do not change mode by themselves. Known mode names are Default and Plan.

## request_user_input availability

Use the `request_user_input` tool only when it is listed in the available tools for this turn.

Use the `request_user_input` tool only for optional questions where the answer would materially improve the quality of the work.

If `request_user_input` returns no answers, continue with best judgment instead of asking again or treating the turn as blocked.

Never use the `request_user_input` tool for permission requests or permission-related escalations.
</collaboration_mode>

## developer

<multi_agent_role>You are `/root`, the primary agent in a team of agents collaborating to fulfill the user's goals.

At the start of your turn, you are the active agent.
You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents.
All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent without triggering a turn.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.
You can decide how much context you want to propagate to your sub-agents with the `fork_turns` parameter.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
They may be addressed as to=/root

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.</multi_agent_role>

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

## Shared Windows CLI tools

On Joshua's Windows laptop, `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` are shared Windows user installs. Read `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\references\ai-workspace\windows-cli-toolchain.md` for their paths, versions, usage, and PATH refresh steps. Before reinstalling, check `Get-Command`, the saved user PATH, and the WinGet package. A running IDE may have stale PATH; refresh the shell or restart that IDE. Keep Python dependencies in project environments.

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Shared Windows CLI tools for Codex, Claude Code, and Antigravity | `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md` — installed paths, PATH refresh, and usage for `uv`, `yt-dlp`, FFmpeg, and Deno |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| Current local work in Claude/Codex cloud | `setup/github-sync/README.md`; private `codex/local-sync/<machine>/<checkout>` branches and generated `.cloud-sync/` metadata/conversation exports. Cloud edits use separate task branches. |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — intake queue; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies clips into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized, so inbox presence alone does not mean unprocessed |
| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

Application repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. Do not create a junction from the vault back into that directory.

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **LSAT teaching:** use project-owned `lsat-u-voice` and `references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md`; this Joshua-selected standard takes precedence over older vendor voice blends.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Shared Windows command tools:** use `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` from the Windows user PATH. For versions, locations, how to invoke them, or a stale terminal environment, read `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md`. Check before reinstalling; restart the affected IDE when its inherited PATH is stale.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.
- **Authorized cloud transfer (2026-10-07):** private sync branches also carry portable skills, memory and conversation exports. Joshua explicitly authorized credential transfer; implementation uses the authenticated encrypted package described in `setup/github-sync/README.md`; plaintext secrets and its unlock key stay outside GitHub and Drive. Cloud authentication and native browser sessions still require separate verification.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

</INSTRUCTIONS>

Use the Node CLI for other supported actions.\r\n---\r\n\r\nFor drills, messages, student data, access, signups, due dates, unassign, and unsend, use `assign-lsatu-work`. On Windows, invoke its Node entry point directly instead of relying on Bash being on PATH.\r\n\r\n\r\n# LSAT U Platform Actions\r\n\r\nJoshua\'s workplace is this brain workspace. LSAT U operations should run headlessly when a supported tool exists. The browser is for checking what a person sees.\r\n\r\n## Mechanism\r\n\r\nFor an interactive HTML lesson, call the `lsat-u-lessons` MCP tool `publish_interactive_lesson` with the student\'s exact email, absolute HTML path, and title. The local stdio server is `system/scripts/lsatu-lesson-mcp.mjs`; Codex registration is `lsat-u-lessons`. It calls `system/scripts/lsatu-admin.mjs post-lesson`, which authenticates with the existing admin credentials, posts through `mcp_post_classroom_note`, verifies the saved HTML exactly and student visibility, and then archives only earlier versions with the same lesson marker. A repeat of the current version returns the same item without another notification. Use `dry_run: true` when the student or replacement is uncertain; otherwise publish in one call. MCP tools newly registered during a session may only appear in a new Codex session; the direct Node command is the immediate fallback.\r\n\r\nThe HTML must be a complete document inside AI OS, under 500 KB, with `<meta name="lsatu-interactive-lesson" content="unique-lesson-id">`. Reuse the same lesson ID for revisions of that lesson; use a different ID for a different lesson. Inline the CSS, JavaScript, fonts, and visual assets. Preflight rejects nonembedded resources; do not bypass it or paste HTML into an ordinary sanitized note. The already-live Classroom renderer loads marked HTML in a sandboxed iframe with scripts and forms enabled. **Daily lesson posting is a content operation and never requires Lovable.** Only an actual app renderer change follows the separate app deployment rules.\r\n\r\nRoutine verification is headless: the publisher reads the new item back, compares its HTML byte for byte, checks that it is visible to the intended student, and confirms one active version. The result includes its route and SHA-256. Use Codex\'s in-app browser only when a visual or interaction defect is suspected or the page design or renderer changed. A teacher login cannot open another student\'s `/classroom/item/<id>` route directly. Do not claim a student-session visual check unless one was actually performed.\r\n\r\nThe student opens the full lesson from Classroom **Notes and recaps**, the in-app notification, or its Inbox link. Classroom Home may show a recent note as a “Read” row, but it has a four-row limit shared with urgent work, so do not promise that every assigned note stays on Home. This does not affect the note\'s visibility in Notes and recaps or its direct item route.\r\n\r\n## UI fallback routing\r\n\r\nThe current student verification route is `/teacher/students/<student-id>/notes`. Its “Posted to their Classroom” section shows active notes and labels archived notes as hidden from the student. Use the browser\'s normal controls for any unsupported action, then verify the saved state through a fresh read. In Codex, use the in-app browser, not Chrome. Do not rely on old `/teacher?ws=` routes or old browser-tool names.\r\n\r\n## Order of operations (every action)\r\n\r\n1. **Identify the student** exactly (roster names include email; ambiguity → one\r\n   concise clarifying question). Check `students/<name>/` for context and\r\n   `assignment-log.md` to avoid repeats.\r\n2. **Do the action** through MCP or the headless Node CLI when supported. Use the UI fallback only for unsupported actions.\r\n3. **Verify it landed**: use the publisher\'s read-after-write result to confirm exact HTML, student visibility, and one active copy of this lesson ID. It archives earlier versions of that ID and leaves unrelated same-title notes alone. Check notifications where available. If verification fails, inspect current items before retrying; never blindly post twice.\r\n4. **Log it**: append to `students/<name>/assignment-log.md` (create if missing) with\r\n   date, action, item, and evidence; drill verifications also go to\r\n   `wiki/guided-drill-verification-log.md`.\r\n\r\n## Guardrails\r\n\r\n- Every action here hits **real students on prod**. Anything a student will see\r\n  (message text, homework instructions, note bodies) is written in Joshua\'s voice —\r\n  route through `wiki/teaching-voice.md`.\r\n- If Joshua\'s request fully specifies the action ("assign Lydia the conditional\r\n  drill"), execute without re-asking. If content is open-ended (a message body he\r\n  hasn\'t dictated), draft it and show him before sending.\r\n- Fail closed: if verification is ambiguous, say exactly what was and wasn\'t\r\n  confirmed. Never report an action complete on the strength of a click alone.\r\n- No student-facing action as a side effect of a different request.\r\n\r\n## Paid session credits\r\n\r\nUse `node system/scripts/lsatu-admin.mjs passes <exact-email>` for balances and `pass-detail` for evidence. An exhausted pass can be replaced with `pass-create <exact-email> --credits N --minutes 70`; dry-run first, then verify through a fresh read. Preserve exhausted passes.\r\n\r\nBefore manually restoring a Stripe package, inspect its `stripe_payments` row and the webhook delivery response. The 2026-09-27 policy returned `grant: awaiting_teacher` for intentional teacher approval. On 2026-10-02, Joshua approved and deployed automatic crediting for new validated, confidently matched package Checkouts, including delayed successes. Existing rows default `auto_grant_eligible=false`; historical, dismissed, and granted payments are not automatically recredited. Unmatched payments stay in review. Verify the live row and deployment before diagnosing a bug; an empty `booking_commerce_events` read is not proof of no payment. Evidence: `research/2026-10-02-celia-payment-crediting.md`.\r\n\r\nAssociate any manual restoration with its exact Stripe payment/event in the booking pass notes. Reconcile the pending teacher grant prompt without adding more credits, using the supported payment-ledger operation after checking its semantics. Do not replay the webhook or invoke `admin_grant_stripe_payment` after manual crediting; either can duplicate sessions. A dismissed payment is not marked granted, and future reconciliation must skip it or explicitly honor the manual fulfillment.\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.9 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Warning: truncated output (original token count: 28568)\nTotal output lines: 711\n\n---\r\nname: assign-lsatu-work\r\ndescription: Use whenever Joshua wants to do anything on LSAT U headlessly, with no browser. That covers assigning an Activity, a guided drill, a homework set, or a timed PT section to one or many students ("assign this to Celia", "give Lydia the Negation Station", "put PT156.S2 on Avneet due Friday", "assign RRC to Celia, Lydia and Dakota"). It covers messaging a student from his admin account ("send Dakota a message", "tell Celia to start with PT105.S2"). It covers reading how students are doing ("how is Celia doing", "what does Lydia have", "who is overdue", "who has gone quiet", "Celia\'s weakest question types", "Avneet\'s last sections"). It covers accounts and upkeep: new signups ("who signed up", "did anyone new join"), access ("give Venusa access", "turn on drills for X", "what can X see"), archiving, moving a due date, unassigning, booking passes ("how many sessions does Sandra have left"), notifications, and reading or marking messages ("who messaged me", "show my thread with Celia"). It is the one place for any operation on the LSAT U platform, and none of it uses Lovable.\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Assign work on LSAT U\r\n\r\nAssign work, message students, and read how they are doing on LSAT U, all\r\nfrom the command line. No browser, no clicking. For complete interactive\r\nClassroom lessons, use the local `lsat-u-lessons` MCP or its direct Node\r\nfallback documented in the AI OS `lsat-u-platform-actions` skill. Both paths\r\npost through LSAT U\'s authenticated API and need no Lovable publish.\r\n\r\n## Authorization\r\n\r\nJoshua pre-authorized this on 2026-09-07: **a single named student is a go.**\r\nRun it, then report. Do not stop to ask permission first.\r\n\r\nConfirm before running when:\r\n\r\n- assigning to **3 or more students at once** — each one emails a real person\r\n- the student name is **ambiguous** (the script refuses and lists the matches)\r\n- the student is **Thaniuska Vivas** or **Amanda** — no platform account found\r\n- Joshua names someone absent from [[STUDENT-PLATFORM-IDENTITY-MAP]]\r\n\r\nThis is a deliberate, narrow exception to his global "draft, never send" rule.\r\nThat rule still holds for email, posts, and anything outside LSAT U.\r\n\r\n## The command\r\n\r\nEverything goes through one command: `system/scripts/lsatu` in the vault\r\n(`C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os`). Run it with bash from the vault root.\r\n\r\n**Never** `cd projects/lsatu` and run `scripts/assign.ts` directly. That\r\ncheckout is on whatever branch another session left it on, sometimes with an\r\nuncommitted `assign.ts` that is missing flags. On 2026-09-23 it had no\r\n`--section`, `--due` or `--set`. The wrapper runs writes from\r\n`projects/.lsatu-ops`, a detached worktree it fast-forwards to `origin/main`\r\nbefore every run. It refuses to run if that copy has local edits.\r\n\r\n**Writes**\r\n\r\n```bash\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu assign --student "Celia" --activity "Read, Recall, Colour"\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu assign --student "Lydia" --drill "main-conclusion-1"\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu assign --student "Dakota" --message "Start with PT105.S2 before Thursday."\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu assign --student "Avneet" --section PT156.S2 --due 2026-09-26\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu assign --student "Avneet" --set "PT156.S2 (LR)"\r\n```\r\n\r\n**Many students at once.** The wrapper resolves every name first, and one bad\r\nname stops the batch before anyone is emailed. The 3+ confirmation rule above\r\nstill applies.\r\n\r\n```bash\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu assign-many "Celia, Lydia, Dakota" --drill "main-conclusion-1" --due 2026-09-26\r\n```\r\n\r\n**Reads.** These never write, so they are safe any time and need no confirmation:\r\n\r\n```bash\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu status "Celia"            # open work, overdue flags, last seen, recent sections\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu qtypes "Celia" --days 30  # accuracy by question type, weakest first\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu sections "Celia"          # recent timed sections with BR scores\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu overdue                   # every past-due unfinished item, all students\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu quiet --days 7            # students with open work, not seen in N days\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu students                  # every account, email, last seen\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu inbox --days 7            # messages students sent you, newest first\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu thread "Celia"            # the full message thread with one student\r\n```\r\n\r\n**Accounts, access, and Classroom upkeep** (`system/scripts/lsatu-admin.mjs`, same\r\nadmin login, calls the same `admin-manage-users` / `booking-pass-admin` edge\r\nfunctions the dashboard calls):\r\n\r\n```bash\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu signups --days 30                  # who joined, and what access each has (NO ACCESS YET = waiting on you)\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu access "Venusa"                    # show every flag, on and off\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu access "Venusa" --template standard   # trial | standard | full, exactly the dashboard templates\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu access "Venusa" --grant drill,activity --revoke chat\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu archive "Name" --reason "finished"   # and: unarchive "Name"\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu items "Celia"                      # her open Classroom items WITH ids\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu due <item-id> 2026-09-30           # move a due date\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu unassign <item-id>                 # hide from her Classroom (archived; her work stays)\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu passes ["Celia"]                   # booking passes: used / total / owed\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu notifications                      # your unread admin notifications\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu read "Celia"                       # mark your thread with her read\r\n```\r\n\r\n**Review queues:**\r\n\r\n```bash\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu drafts [--status draft|approved|posted|all]   # AI TA drafts waiting on you\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu draft <id>                          # full text\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu draft-set <id> approved|rejected|posted\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu draft-send <id>                     # message it to the student, then mark posted\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu uploads [--all]                     # student uploads not yet reviewed\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu upload-review <id> parsed|needs_review|failed\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu notes [--all]                       # session notes waiting on a match\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu note-resolve <ledger-id> "Celia"    # file the note under her\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu note-discard <ledger-id> [--reason ".."]\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu scan-notes                          # scan Drive for new session notes now\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu plans "Lydia"                       # her weekly plans and items\r\n```\r\n\r\n**Unsend a message:**\r\n\r\n```bash\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu thread "Adriana" --ids          # message ids\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu unsend <message-id> [--dry-run]\r\nsystem/scripts/lsatu unsend-last "Adriana" [--dry-run]   # your most recent message to her\r\n```\r\n\r\nThis removes only your own messages, never a student\'s. It reports whether\r\nshe has already opened the thread: if she has not, she never sees the\r\nmessage. A plain message makes no bell entry and no email, so removing it\r\nremoves every trace. An assignment\'s email cannot be recalled, and unsending\r\nits inbox message does not unassign the work; use `unassign` for that.\r\nDeleting is permanent, so confirm with Joshua every time unless he named the\r\nmessage in this turn.\r\n\r\n**Multi-line text is refused on purpose.** On Windows the npx shim cuts an\r\nargument at the first newline and drops everything after it, `--dry-run`\r\nincluded. On 2026-09-23 a "dry run" of `draft-send` really sent Adriana Varea\r\nthe first line of a draft. The wrapper now refuses any multi-line argument.\r\nMessages must be one line until `assign.ts` reads its text from a file.\r\n\r\nEvery write here takes `--dry-run`. Flag short names: practice, drill,\r\nclassroom, bootcamp, analytics, schedule, waj, journal, flagged, chat, export,\r\nta, activity. Templates always leave the "paying student" defaults on, the\r\nsame as the dashboard does.\r\n\r\n**Go without asking:** every read, `access` grants for one named student,\r\n`due`, and `read`. **Confirm first:** `archive`, `unarchive`, `unassign`,\r\n`--all-off`, and any `--revoke`, because each of these takes something away\r\nfrom a real student. **Not available here on purpose:** deleting a student\r\n(permanent), changing admin roles, and anything that edits app code or the\r\ndatabase schema. Those stay with Joshua in the dashboard or Lovable.\r\n\r\n**Other platform jobs that already have a home:** one-off booking links and\r\nnew passes go through the `booking-link` skill (`scripts/booking-link.ts`).\r\nAccommodations emails use the `accommodations-email` skill.\r\n\r\nAdd `--json` to any read when you want to compute on the output. `overdue` and\r\n`quiet` include former students, because the platform has no "former" flag.\r\nCross-check `students/roster.generated.md` before acting on either list.\r\n\r\nWhen you are unsure what a student already has, run `status` before\r\nassigning. One call catches both double-assigns and the overdue pile.\r\n\r\nFlags: `--schedule YYYY-MM-DD` unlocks the work that morning. `--due YYYY-MM-DD`\r\nis due at the end of that local day and shows on the card and the calendar\r\nstrip. `--dry-run` resolves the student and the work and writes nothing — use it\r\nwhen the name is at all uncertain.\r\n\r\n**Dates are absolute.** "Due Saturday" means look up the date of the coming\r\nSaturday and pass that. On 2026-09-10 (a Thursday) Saturday was 2026-09-12; a\r\nhandoff once carried 09-13, which is the Sunday.\r\n\r\nTo pace a student over several days, run the command once per day with a\r\n`--schedule` on each. Each run is its own assignment, its own row, and its own\r\nemail, so she is told about each day rather than handed a block.\r\n\r\nEvery assignment lands on the home calendar strip and the cell opens that\r\nassignment — scheduled work on its unlock day, unscheduled work on the day you\r\nassigned it. Nothing needs to be written by hand to put work on the calendar;\r\nif an assignment is missing from the band, that is a bug to fix in\r\n`useClassroomHome`, not something to patch into `classroom_items`.\r\n\r\nAlways `--dry-run` first for a student you have not assigned to before in this\r\nsession. It costs one second and it is the only cheap way to catch a wrong\r\naccount.\r\n\r\n## Why a script and not a direct DB write\r\n\r\n`scripts/assign.ts` calls the app\'s **own** `assignWork()` from\r\n`src/lib/assignWork.ts`. Assigning touches seven tables — the assignment\r\nsnapshot, `classroom_items`, the conversation and its participants, `messages`,\r\n`notifications` — plus the email edge function, and it rolls the whole thing\r\nback if the classroom item or notify throws. There is no RPC and no edge\r\nfunction for it; it is client-side code.\r\n\r\nSo never hand-write these rows. A second implementation drifts from the UI the\r\nfirst time the app changes, and produces assignments subtly unlike the ones\r\nJoshua makes by clicking.\r\n\r\n## Credentials\r\n\r\nRead from `~/ai-os/.env`, which is **not** a git repo:\r\n\r\n- `LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL`, `LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD` — already set\r\n\r\nNever put a credential in `projects/lsatu/.env`. That file **is tracked in git**\r\nand would be committed. The script signs in as Joshua and works under normal\r\nadmin RLS, the same permission path the UI uses.\r\n\r\n`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` is deliberately **not** used even when present: it\r\nbypasses RLS on every write, so a script running as service_role would hide an\r\nRLS regression instead of hitting it. The script warns and falls back.\r\n\r\n## Expected output\r\n\r\n```\r\nAssign "Read, Recall, Colour" → Celia <celiacutler@gmail.com>\r\nAssigned. id=e83e2193-…\r\n  inbox ok · bell ok · email ok\r\n```\r\n\r\nA notify channel reading `FAILED` is **not** an assignment failure — the work is\r\nin her Classroom and only a message went missing. Report it, do not re-run.\r\n\r\n## Failure modes\r\n\r\n**`duplicate key … activity_assignments_active_unique`** — she already holds an\r\nactive assignment for this Activity. Not a bug; the DB refusing a double-assign.\r\nCheck what she has before re-trying (below).\r\n\r\n**`"X" is ambiguous`** — the script lists every matching account and stops.\r\nPick the full name or the email. Never guess: a misdirected assignment is\r\nsilent, because she simply never sees it.\r\n\r\n**Sign-in failed** — the credentials in `~/ai-os/.env` are wrong or the account\r\ngained MFA.\r\n\r\n## Checking what a student already has\r\n\r\n`system/scripts/lsatu status "Celia"` lists every open classroom item with its\r\ntype, status, assign date and due date, and flags anything overdue.\r\n\r\n## A PT section as timed homework\r\n\r\n`--section PT156.S2` (also accepts `PT156-S2`, `pt 156 s 2`) assigns one\r\nLogical Reasoning section to run under the real 35:00 section clock, with the\r\nofficial PrepTest header, exactly like a section inside a practice test.\r\n\r\nWhat the script does, verified end to end on 2026-09-10 on\r\n`joshua.fisseha01@gmail.com` (the test account):\r\n\r\n1. Reads `public/data/PT156-S2-LR.json` and builds the qids\r\n   `PT156-S2-Q1 … Q25` in order. No file → it lists the sections that PT does\r\n   have and stops. Only LR sections are loaded; there is no RC/LG file set.\r\n2. Finds or creates the homework set titled **`PT156.S2 (LR)`** with exactly\r\n   those qids and `estimated_minutes = 35`. One set per section, reused; a\r\n   same-title set with different questions stops the run.\r\n3. Calls the app\'s own `assignWork({ kind: \'homework_set\' })`: assignment row,\r\n   classroom item (with `due_at`), inbox message, bell, email.\r\n4. Turns on `has_classroom_access` and `has_drill_access` if either was off,\r\n   because the set opens at `/classroom/:id` and runs at `/drill`.\r\n\r\nExpected output:\r\n\r\n```\r\nAssign "PT156.S2 (LR)" → josh <joshua.fisseha01@gmail.com>  (due 2026-09-13)\r\n  25 questions, timed 35:00 · set reused (ff0fe127-…)\r\nAssigned. id=04e154c8-…\r\n  inbox ok · bell ok · email ok\r\n```\r\n\r\n**Already assigned:** the script says\r\n`already has "PT156.S2 (LR)" (in_progress, assigned 2026-09-10)` and stops. No browser.\r\n#\r\n#   lsatu assign --student "Celia" --section PT156.S2 --due 2026-09-26 [--dry-run]\r\n#   lsatu assign-many "Celia, Lydia, Dakota" --drill "main-conclusion-1" [--dry-run]\r\n#   lsatu status "Celia" | qtypes "Celia" [--days 30] | sections "Celia"\r\n#   lsatu overdue | quiet [--days 7] | students | inbox [--days 7] | thread "Celia"   (reads: --json)\r\n#   lsatu signups | access "Name" [--template standard | --grant x,y | --revoke z] | archive | unarchive\r\n#   lsatu items "Name" | due <item-id> YYYY-MM-DD | unassign <item-id> | passes | notifications | read "Name"\r\n#\r\n# Writes run from the applications root/.lsatu-ops, a detached worktree pinned to\r\n# origin/main and fast-forwarded before every run, so another session\'s branch\r\n# or uncommitted edits in the lsatu repository can never change what "assign" does.\r\nset -euo pipefail\r\nROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/../.." && pwd)"\r\nPROJECTS="$(dirname "$ROOT")/ai-os-projects"\r\n[ -d "$PROJECTS" ] || PROJECTS="$ROOT/projects"\r\nMAIN="$PROJECTS/lsatu"\r\nOPS="$PROJECTS/.lsatu-ops"\r\n\r\nsync_ops() {\r\n  if [ ! -d "$OPS" ]; then\r\n    git -C "$MAIN" fetch -q origin main\r\n    git -C "$MAIN" worktree add -q --detach "$OPS" origin/main\r\n  fi\r\n  if [ ! -e "$OPS/node_modules" ]; then\r\n    cmd //c "mklink /J $(cygpath -w "$OPS/node_modules") $(cygpath -w "$MAIN/node_modules")" >/dev/null\r\n  fi\r\n  # vite-node runs the @lovable.dev/mcp-js Vite plugin, which rewrites this\r\n  # generated entry with a local path on every run. "");\r\n  if (!file.startsWith(ROOT + path.sep) || path.extname(file).toLowerCase() !== ".html") {\r\n    throw new Error("Lesson must be an HTML file inside the AI OS workspace");\r\n  }\r\n  const html = fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8");\r\n  if (Buffer.byteLength(html) > 500000) throw new Error("Lesson exceeds the 500 KB post limit");\r\n  const lessonId = validateInteractiveLesson(html);\r\n  const title = flag("title", null)?.trim();\r\n  if (!title) throw new Error("post-lesson requires --title");\r\n  const lock = path.join(os.tmpdir(), `lsatu-lesson-${createHash("sha256").update(u.class_id).update(lessonId).digest("hex")}.lock`);\r\n  const start = Date.now();\r\n  while (true) {\r\n    try { fs.mkdirSync(lock); break; }\r\n    catch (error) {\r\n      if (error.code !== "EEXIST" || Date.now() - start > 30000) throw new Error("Another publish is running for this student and lesson; retry after it finishes");\r\n      await new Promise((done) => setTimeout(done, 150));\r\n    }\r\n  }\r\n  try {\r\n    const rows = await rest(`classroom_items?student_id=eq.${u.class_id}&type=eq.note&title=eq.${encodeURIComponent(title)}&select=id,type,title,status,visible_to_student,content_html&order=created_at.desc&limit=1000`);\r\n    const sameLesson = rows.filter((item) => item.content_html && lessonMarkerId(item.content_html) === lessonId);\r\n    const previous = sameLesson.filter((item) => item.status !== "archived" && item.visible_to_student);\r\n    console.log(`${nameOf(u)} <${u.email}>: post interactive lesson "${title}" [${lessonId}] (${Buffer.byteLength(html)} bytes)`);\r\n    console.log(`  previous versions: ${previous.map((i) => i.id).join(", ") || "none"}`);\r\n    if (dry) { console.log("(dry run — nothing changed)"); inspect the student\'s items before retrying");\r\n      console.log(JSON.stringify({ ...result, lesson_id: lessonId, archived_previous: archived, verified: true, html_sha256: createHash("sha256").update(html).digest("hex") }, null, 2));\r\n    }\r\n  } finally { fs.rmdirSync(lock); }\r\n} else if (cmd === "signups") {\r\n  const days = Number(flag("days", 30));\r\n  const since = new Date(Date.now() - days * 864e5).toISOString();\r\n  const [notes, all] = await Promise.all([\r\n    rest(`notifications?user_id=eq.${ADMIN_ID}&type=eq.signup&created_at=gte.${since}&select=id,body,read,created_at&order=created_at.desc`),\r\n    users(),\r\n  ]);\r\n    const band = level ? catalog.bands[level] : [1, 5];\r\n    const difficulties = Object.entries(d.pool_summary.difficulty_counts).filter(([difficulty, count]) => count > 0 && Number(difficulty) >= band[0] && Number(difficulty) <= band[1]);\r\n    return { min: difficulties.length ? Number(difficulties[0][0]) : null, max: difficulties.length ? Number(difficulties.at(-1)[0]) : null, count: difficulties.reduce((sum, [, count]) => sum + count, 0) };\r\n  }\r\n  const matching = catalog.drills.filter(d => {\r\n    const selected = eligible(d);\r\n    return d.is_published && (include_pinned || !d.pool_summary.pinned_qids) && !d.pool_summary.missing_pinned_qids.length && words.every(word => JSON.stringify([d.slug, d.title, d.bank_filter, d.reasoning_types]).toLowerCase().includes(word)) && selected.count >= (d.pool_summary.pinned_qids || d.question_count || 1) && (min_difficulty == null || (selected.min != null && selected.min >= min_difficulty)) && (max_difficulty == null || (selected.max != null && selected.max <= max_difficulty));\r\n  }).sort((a, b) => Number(words.every(word => b.title.toLowerCase().includes(word))) - Number(words.every(word => a.title.toLowerCase().includes(word))));\r\n  const result = { snapshot: catalog.meta.generated_at, total_matches: matching.length, drills: matching.slice(0, limit).map(d => ({ slug: d.slug, title: d.title, count: d.pool_summary.pinned_qids || d.question_count, minutes: d.estimated_minutes, catalog_level: d.level, native_band: [d.bank_filter.difficulty_min, d.bank_filter.difficulty_max], selectable_at_level: d.pool_summary.pinned_qids ? \'exact pins; level ignored\' : level ?? \'native\', eligible: eligible(d), pool_difficulty_counts: d.pool_summary.difficulty_counts, pinned_count: d.pool_summary.pinned_qids })) };\r\n  return { content: [{ type: \'text\', text: JSON.stringify(result) }], structuredContent: result };\r\n});\r\n\r\nserver.registerTool(\'preview_guided_drill_batch\', {\r\n  title: \'Preflight a guided drill schedule\',\r\n  description: \'Read-only live preflight for one student and up to 14 guided assignments. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.7 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'import fs from \'node:fs\';\r\nimport path from \'node:path\';\r\nimport { fileURLToPath } from \'node:url\';\r\n\r\nexport const vaultRoot = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), \'../..\');\r\nconst sibling = path.join(path.dirname(vaultRoot), \'ai-os-projects\');\r\nexport const projectsRoot = fs.existsSync(sibling) ? sibling : path.join(vaultRoot, \'projects\');\r\nexport const projectPath = (...parts) => path.join(projectsRoot, ...parts);\r\n# Assign an Activity — the runbook\r\n\r\nThe repeatable path for putting an LSAT U Activity into a student\'s hands.\r\nWritten 2026-09-07 after walking it live on lsatprep.study.\r\n\r\nJoshua says **"assign &lt;activity&gt; to &lt;student&gt;"** and this is what happens,\r\nend to end, with no further questions in the normal case.\r\n\r\nCompanion: [[STUDENT-PLATFORM-IDENTITY-MAP]] — which platform account is which\r\nstudent. Read it before ticking a name.\r\n\r\n## Standing authorization\r\n\r\nJoshua granted this on 2026-09-07: assigning an Activity to a **named student**\r\nis a **go** — do it, then report. Do not stop to ask permission first.\r\n\r\nThis is a deliberate exception to the global "draft, never send" rule, which\r\nstill holds for email, posts, and messages. It exists because assignment is the\r\ncore loop of the tutoring business and confirming each one made it useless.\r\n\r\n**Still confirm first when:**\r\n\r\n- assigning to **3 or more students at once** (each one emails a real person)\r\n- the name is **ambiguous** — most importantly **Steven Morris vs "Stevie"**,\r\n  two accounts that may be one person\r\n- the student is on the unresolved list: **Thaniuska Vivas** (no account),\r\n  **Amanda** (no account found)\r\n- Joshua names a student who is **not in the identity map at all**\r\n\r\n## The path\r\n\r\nThe Activity Centre lives at `/activities` — Classroom sidebar → **Study** →\r\n**Activities**. Joshua\'s account is admin, so each card in **The library** carries\r\nits own assign button.\r\n\r\n1. Navigate to `https://lsatprep.study/activities`\r\n2. `find` the button **"Assign &lt;Activity name&gt;"** (one per card, exact title)\r\n3. Click it — a dialog opens, titled **"Assign: &lt;Activity name&gt;"**\r\n4. Optional: fill **"Schedule for"** (a date input) if Joshua named a due date\r\n5. `read_page` the dialog and locate the student\'s checkbox by display name\r\n6. Click the checkbox — the footer button updates to **"Assign to 1 students"**\r\n7. Click that button\r\n8. Confirm the result, then report what was sent\r\n\r\nThe dialog is a portal — it renders **outside `<main>`**, so `get_page_text` will\r\nnot show it. Use `read_page` (with the dialog\'s `ref_id` to keep output small).\r\n\r\n## The nine Activities\r\n\r\nAssign by the exact card title:\r\n\r\n| # | World | Activity | Grain |\r\n|---|---|---|---|\r\n| 01 | The Specimen Lab | Vocab Rehab | a word |\r\n| 02 | The Signal Observatory | Spot the Relationship | a sentence |\r\n| 03 | The Switchyard | Conditional Rephrase | a statement, translated |\r\n| 04 | The Polarity Chamber | Negation Station | a statement, negated |\r\n| 05 | The Argument Atelier | Find the Conclusion | an argument |\r\n| 06 | The Debate Chamber | Rebuttal Rephrase | an argument, restated |\r\n| 07 | The Press | Dumb it Down | a paragraph |\r\n| 08 | The Loom | Spot the Callback | a passage |\r\n| 09 | Out Loud | Read, Recall, Colour | a whole section, against the clock |\r\n\r\n### Read, Recall, Colour\r\n\r\nAssign **card 09** — the section-library door. The student then chooses any of\r\nthe 12 seeded sections, each 25 minutes:\r\n\r\nPT1.S3 · PT21.S3 · PT101.S2 · PT102.S3 · PT103.S1 · PT104.S1 · PT105.S1 ·\r\nPT105.S2 · PT106.S2 · PT112.S4 · PT134.S3 · PT140.S1\r\n\r\nEvery attempt is kept. Re-running a section saves a **new attempt**; it never\r\nreplaces the old one.\r\n\r\nOnly assign an individual `read-recall-colour-pt*-s*` slug when Joshua\r\ndeliberately wants her pinned to one section. The door is the default.\r\n\r\n## What assigning actually does\r\n\r\n`assignWork()` — `projects/lsatu/src/lib/assignWork.ts`:\r\n\r\n1. **Snapshots** the Activity into `activity_assignments`. The snapshot is\r\n   immutable once issued, so later content edits do not reach an assignment\r\n   already sent.\r\n2. **Writes a `classroom_items` row** — this is what makes it reachable at all.\r\n3. **Notifies on four channels**: inbox message, bell, email, classroom item.\r\n\r\nNotification failure never fails the assignment. A student can therefore hold an\r\nassignment she was never told about — worth checking if she says she got nothing.\r\n\r\n## Verifying it landed\r\n\r\nAfter assigning, the sheet closes and a toast confirms. To verify properly:\r\nopen `/teacher` → the student\'s record, or have her Classroom show the item\r\nunder **Assigned work**.\r\n\r\nIf she says she cannot see it: check her **Activities access flag** first.\r\nFlags resolve in `projects/lsatu/src/hooks/useUserPermissions.ts`. An assignment\r\nlands regardless of flags, but the surface that displays it may be gated.\r\n\r\n## Defects to mention when assigning Read, Recall, Colour\r\n\r\nAs of 2026-09-07 these are live and unfixed. Tell her, or fix them first:\r\n\r\n- The door\'s heading reads **"Recall Rephrase Colour"** — a name that is not the\r\n  product\'s — directly under a pill reading "Read, Recall, Colour".\r\n  Hardcoded at `SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:281`; the aria-label at :314 repeats it;\r\n  `SectionLibraryLaunch.test.tsx:54` asserts the wrong string, which is why the\r\n  2,643-test suite stays green.\r\n- Step 1\'s copy in production is the old *"Intake only…"* wording. The rewrite\r\n  and its migration `20260906220000_simplify_recall_read_step.sql` are\r\n  uncommitted and have never run.\r\n- Attempt dates can render out of order — PT134.S3 shows Attempt 1 dated after\r\n  Attempt 3. This is the `graded_at` vs `created_at` bug named in `PLAN.md`\r\n  item 5.\r\n\r\n## Verified working (2026-09-07)\r\n\r\n- All 9 Activities seeded and listed\r\n- All 12 RRC sections seeded and runnable\r\n- `attempt_no` **is** applied in production — multi-attempt history renders\r\n- 33 accounts in the picker; 20 map to real students\r\n\r\nRelated: [[STUDENT-PLATFORM-IDENTITY-MAP]] · [[tasks]] · [[hot]]\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.7 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '/**\r\n * Headless assign: put an Activity, a guided drill, or a plain message into a\r\n * student\'s LSAT U account from the command line.\r\n *\r\n * Run with vite-node so `@/` resolves and this calls the app\'s OWN assignWork()\r\n * rather than a second implementation of it. That matters: assigning touches\r\n * seven tables (assignment snapshot, classroom item, conversation, message,\r\n * notification, plus the email edge function) and rolls back on failure. A\r\n * reimplementation would drift from the UI the first time the app changed.\r\n *\r\n *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --activity "Read, Recall, Colour"\r\n *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --drill "main-conclusion-1"\r\n *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --message "Start with PT105.S2."\r\n *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --section PT156.S2 --due 2026-09-13\r\n *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --set "PT156.S2 (LR)"\r\n *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --activity "..." --dry-run\r\n *\r\n * Credentials are read from ~/ai-os/.env (git-ignored), never from this repo\'s\r\n * .env, which IS tracked in git. Same convention as scripts/db-pull.mjs.\r\n */\r\nimport fs from "node:fs";\r\nimport path from "node:path";\r\nimport { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";\r\nimport { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";\r\nimport { assignWork } from "@/lib/assignWork";\r\n\r\nconst REPO = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");\r\n\r\nfunction readEnv(file: string): Record<string, string> {\r\n  const out: Record<string, string> = {};\r\n  if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return out;\r\n  for (const line of fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8").split(/\\r?\\n/)) {\r\n    const t = line.trim();\r\n    if (!t || t.startsWith("#")) continue;\r\n    const i = t.indexOf("=");\r\n    if (i < 0) continue;\r\n    out[t.slice(0, i).trim()] = t.slice(i + 1).trim().replace(/^["\']|["\']$/g, "");\r\n  }\r\n  return out;\r\n}\r\n\r\nconst env = {\r\n  ...readEnv(path.resolve(REPO, "../../.env")),\r\n  ...readEnv(path.join(REPO, ".env")),\r\n};\r\n\r\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------- arguments\r\n\r\nfunction arg(name: string): string | undefined {\r\n  const i = process.argv.indexOf(`--${name}`);\r\n  return i >= 0 ? process.argv[i + 1] : undefined;\r\n}\r\nconst has = (name: string) => process.argv.includes(`--${name}`);\r\n\r\nconst studentQuery = arg("student");\r\nconst activityQuery = arg("activity");\r\nconst drillQuery = arg("drill");\r\nconst messageText = arg("message");\r\nconst sectionQuery = arg("section");\r\nconst setQuery = arg("set");\r\nconst scheduleFor = arg("schedule");\r\nconst dueOn = arg("due");\r\nconst dryRun = has("dry-run");\r\n\r\nconst kinds = [activityQuery, drillQuery, messageText, sectionQuery, setQuery].filter(Boolean).length;\r\nif (!studentQuery || kinds !== 1) {\r\n  console.error(\r\n    "usage: vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student <name|email> \\\\\\n" +\r\n      "         (--activity <title> | --drill <slug|title> | --message <text>\\\\\\n" +\r\n      "          | --section PT<n>.S<n> | --set <title|id>)\\\\\\n" +\r\n      "         [--schedule YYYY-MM-DD] [--due YYYY-MM-DD] [--dry-run]",\r\n  );\r\n  process.exit(2);\r\n}\r\nfor (const [flag, v] of [["schedule", scheduleFor], ["due", dueOn]] as const) {\r\n  if (v && !/^\\d{4}-\\d{2}-\\d{2}$/.test(v)) {\r\n    console.error(`--${flag} must be YYYY-MM-DD, got "${v}"`);\r\n    process.exit(2);\r\n  }\r\n}\r\nif (dueOn && scheduleFor && dueOn < scheduleFor) {\r\n  console.error(`--due ${dueOn} is before --schedule ${scheduleFor}; she could never do it in time.`);\r\n  process.exit(2);\r\n}\r\n\r\n// --------------------------------------------------------------- signing in\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Sign in as Joshua. The service_role key would bypass RLS entirely, so it is\r\n * only honoured when explicitly present; the default is a normal admin login,\r\n * which exercises the same permission path the UI does. A script that quietly\r\n * ran as service_role would hide an RLS regression instead of hitting it.\r\n */\r\nasync function signIn(): Promise<string> {\r\n  if (env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY) {\r\n    console.warn(\r\n      "! SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY is set but this script signs in as the admin user.\\n" +\r\n        "  Using it would require injecting a second client into assignWork, which would\\n" +\r\n        "  bypass RLS for every write. Falling back to the admin login.",\r\n    );\r\n  }\r\n  const email = env.LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL;\r\n  const password = [REDACTED];\r\n  if (!email || !password) {\r\n    console.error(\r\n      "assign: LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL / LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD are not set.\\n" +\r\n        "  Add them to ~/ai-os/.env (git-ignored). This repo\'s .env is TRACKED in git,\\n" +\r\n        "  so a credential placed there would be committed.",\r\n    );\r\n    process.exit(2);\r\n  }\r\n  const { data, error } = await supabase.auth.signInWithPassword({ email, password });\r\n  if (error || !data.user) {\r\n    console.error(`assign: sign-in failed — ${error?.message ?? "no user returned"}`);\r\n    process.exit(1);\r\n  }\r\n  return data.user.id;\r\n}\r\n\r\n// -------------------------------------------------------------- resolution\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 2.6 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '  if [ ! -e "$OPS/node_modules" ]; then\r\n    cmd //c "mklink /J $(cygpath -w "$OPS/node_modules") $(cygpath -w "$MAIN/node_modules")" >/dev/null\r\n  fi\r\n  # vite-node runs the @lovable.dev/mcp-js Vite plugin, which rewrites this\r\n  # generated entry with a local path on every run. Put the committed one back.\r\n  git -C "$OPS" restore supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts 2>/dev/null || true\r\n  if [ -n "$(git -C "$OPS" status --porcelain --untracked-files=no)" ]; then\r\n    echo "lsatu: $OPS has local edits; it must stay a clean copy of main. Not running." >&2\r\n    exit 1\r\n  fi\r\n  git -C "$OPS" fetch -q origin main\r\n  git -C "$OPS" checkout -q --detach origin/main\r\n}\r\n\r\nrun_assign() {\r\n  local rc=0\r\n  # On Windows the npx shim splits argv at a newline: everything after it,\r\n  # including --dry-run, is silently dropped. That sent a real message on\r\n  # 2026-09-23. Refuse multi-line arguments until assign.ts reads text from a file.\r\n  for a in "$@"; do\r\n    case "$a" in *$\'\\n\'*|*$\'\\r\'*) echo "lsatu: refusing a multi-line argument (Windows would drop everything after the first line, including --dry-run). Nothing sent." >&2; return 2;; esac\r\n  done\r\n  # Keep the pinned worktree clean while pointing its credential reader at the\r\n  # separate knowledge vault. This ignored copy contains a path, never secrets.\r\n  node "$ROOT/system/scripts/prepare-assign.mjs" "$OPS"\r\n  (cd "$OPS" && npx --no-install vite-node scripts/.ai-os-assign.generated.ts -- "$@") || rc=$?\r\n  git -C "$OPS" restore supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts 2>/dev/null || true\r\n  return $rc\r\n}\r\n\r\ncmd="${1:-}"; shift || true\r\ncase "$cmd" in\r\n  assign)\r\n    sync_ops\r\n    run_assign "$@"\r\n    ;;\r\n  assign-many)\r\n    list="${1:?name the students, comma-separated}"; shift\r\n    sync_ops\r\n    IFS=\',\' read -ra names <<< "$list"\r\n    # Resolve every name first. One bad name stops the batch before anyone is emailed.\r\n    for n in "${names[@]}"; do\r\n      n="$(echo "$n" | xargs)"\r\n      run_assign --student "$n" "$@" --dry-run >/dev/null || { echo "lsatu: stopped, \\"$n\\" did not resolve. Nothing was assigned." >&2; exit 1; }\r\n    done\r\n    for a in "$@"; do [ "$a" = "--dry-run" ] && { echo "all ${#names[@]} resolved (dry run)"; exit 0; }; done\r\n    fail=0\r\n    for n in "${names[@]}"; do\r\n      n="$(echo "$n" | xargs)"\r\n      echo "== $n"\r\n      run_assign --student "$n" "$@" || fail=$((fail+1))\r\n    done\r\n    [ "$fail" -eq 0 ] || { echo "lsatu: $fail of ${#names[@]} failed; see above." >&2; exit 1; }\r\n    ;;\r\n  draft-send)\r\n    # Send an AI TA draft to its student as an admin message, then mark it posted.\r\n    id="${1:?draft id}"; shift\r\n    json="$(node "$ROOT/system/scripts/lsatu-admin.mjs" draft "$id" --json)"\r\n    email="$(printf \'%s\' "$json" | node -e \'let s="";process.stdin.on("data",d=>s+=d).on("end",()=>{const d=JSON.parse(s);if(!d.student_email)process.exit(1);process.stdout.write(d.student_email)})\')"\r\n    body="$(printf \'%s\' "$json" | node -e \'let s="";process.stdin.on("data",d=>s+=d).on("end",()=>process.stdout.write(JSON.parse(s).draft_markdown||""))\')"\r\n    [ -n "$body" ] || { echo "lsatu: draft $id has no text to send." >&2; exit 1; }\r\n    sync_ops\r\n    run_assign --student "$email" --message "$body" "$@"\r\n    for a in "$@"; do [ "$a" = "--dry-run" ] && exit 0; done\r\n    node "$ROOT/system/scripts/lsatu-admin.mjs" draft-set "$id" posted\r\n    ;;\r\n  signups|access|tier|archive|unarchive|items|post-lesson|due|unassign|passes|pass-detail|pass-create|credit|notifications|read|drafts|draft-set|uploads|upload-review|notes|note-resolve|note-discard|scan-notes|plans|unsend|unsend-last)\r\n    node "$ROOT/system/scripts/lsatu-admin.mjs" "$cmd" "$@"\r\n    ;;\r\n  status|qtypes|sections|overdue|quiet|students|inbox|thread)\r\n    node "$ROOT/system/scripts/lsatu-report.mjs" "$cmd" "$@"\r\n    ;;\r\n  *)\r\n    sed -n \'2,10p\' "$0" >&2\r\n    exit 2\r\n    ;;\r\nesac\r\n'}]

Show each complete QUESTION\'s stimulus, stem, and five choices, with its original frame available to enlarge. Copy-question text must exclude answers and teacher notes. Put a suggested editable 120-second work timer before each question\'s walkthrough; identify this as teacher pacing, not a claimed source timing.
